4.41 The Committee observes that out of 285 sanctioned posts in 37 functional Super
Sl. No. Department Sanctioned Filled

53
Specialties Departments at the AIIMS Jodhpur, only 142 seats have been filled up which
constitutes only 50% of the sanctioned seats. The Committee is appalled to note that the
Departments of Cardiology, Cardiothoracic Surgery, Endocrinology and Metabolism, General
Medicine, Nephrology, Neurology, Surgical Oncology and Radio Therapy are functioning with
single person manning these Departments.  Further, in the Neonatology Department, there
are only two faculty and one of them is working on contractual basis. These Departments
cater to cases of heart, kidney, and neurological disease. The Committee is of the view that
Super Specialty Departments offer specialized diagnosis and treatment protocol dealing with
a particular disease as it is not possible for a general physician to deal with patients
suffering from all kinds of diseases. The gap in the actual position and the sanctioned
strength of Super Specialists will adversely affect the working efficiency of the Institute as
patients would be denied the services of the institute due to lack of Specialty/Super Specialty
Departments. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Institute must fill up the
vacant faculty posts at the Professor/HOD level so as to provide best services to the patients
visiting the Institute.
Medical Education
4.42 AIIMS, Jodhpur started its academic journey on 17th September, 2012 with the first batch
of 50 students joining the MBBS programme. From the year 2013, each batch has 100 students
joining the MBBS course. The College of Nursing started functioning from 2nd September, 2013
with intake of 60 students each year. The Institute has started Post Graduate and Super Specialty
courses in 33 Departments. Besides, Certificate Courses and Fellowships in various Departments
have also been started and also in the College of Nursing. A Ph.D. programme has also commenced
at AIIMS, Jodhpur. Recently a Master of Public Health course has also been introduced in School
of Public Health of the Institute.
4.43 In PG courses at AIIMS, Jodhpur there are 114 sanctioned PG seats in 25 different Departments
and 84 seats have been filled up in the year 2018. The Committee has been informed that PG
courses (MD/MS/MDS/DM/MCh) in the following subjects have been approved:-
Sl. No.  Name of Subject Sanctioned Seats
1. PMR (MD) 2 Per Year
2. Endocrinology and Metabolism (DM) 2 Per Year
3. Infectious Diseases (DM) 4 Per Year
4. Clinical Pharmacology (DM) 1 Per Year
5. Trauma Surgery and Critical Care (MCh) 2 Per Year
4.44 The Committee notes that in AIIMS, Jodhpur there has been 100% admissions in
MBBS and nursing courses. Meanwhile, the number of seats in PG course has increased
from 56 to 114 in the year 2018.  However, out of 114 seats, only 84 seats have been filled
up. The Ministry has not furnished reasons to the Committee as to why the remaining PG
course seats could not be filled up. The Committee would like the Ministry to furnish a
note apprising reasons for not filling up of PG course seats along with steps taken to

54
address faculty shortage and other benchmarks required for a Medical college of AIIMS
standard.
4.45 As regards clinical training of MBBS/Nursing graduates, the Ministry informed that a structured
programme of 3 weeks was held for 3rd and 4th Semester MBBS students on the contextual
learning of two Professional subjects with the patient at the centre stage. Students were sensitized
to acquire skills in eliciting clinical history, examination techniques and patient care with focus on
communication skills. They were also trained for electronic record keeping through AIIMS Jodhpur’s
unique Computerized Patient Management System (CPMS). Students of 2012 batch were trained in
all clinical Departments in Para-clinical subjects (Pathology, Pharmacology, Microbiology and Forensic
Medicine). IPD offered medical students extensive opportunity to devote more time in honing their
clinical skills. The IPD services were linked with Clinical Laboratory Services and Diagnostic
Radiology including X-rays and USG which were all developed in-house.
4.46 The Committee apprehends that inadequate faculty as well as limited clinical training
could be one of the reasons for PG seats not being filled up in the year 2018. The Committee
believes that exposure of students to proper clinical training would help enriching their
skills and experience in medical education. The Committee, therefore, expects the Institute
to improve the level of its faculty as well as clinical standards and ensure that from next
session, henceforth, that all the PG seats are filled up. The Committee also recommends to
the Institute to start the newly approved PG courses after assessing availability of adequate
faculty and requisite patient load.  The Committee has been apprised of a abysmally low
level of clinical exposure to PG students in AIIMS, Jodhpur specifically MD (Anesthesiology).
The Committee recommends that a quarterly status note be submitted to the Committee on
the clinical exposure of PG Students (discipline-wise) so as to ensure that educational standards
are not diluted.
4.47 The Committee opines that an effective and relevant training module expands the
capacity building of the medical and nursing students and it therefore, plays an important
role in their career development. Clinical trainings help the healthcare professionals to
better understand the subject and prepare them for the challenges they will face in their
career. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Institute must start clinical training
programmes for the students and ensure that there is adequate faculty at the Institute to
support these programmes.
Status of Operation Theatres
4.48 The Ministry has submitted that AIIMS Jodhpur has 33 sanctioned Operation Theatres. Currently
9 major and 3 minor operation theatres are fully functional. Out of these 3 major and 2 minor OTs
(in Trauma OT complex) do not have medical gas pipe line and the supply of gas is ensured using
cylinders. Rest of OTs have fully functional medical gas pipe line and adequate manpower. The
medical gas pipe line in the hospital including the Trauma OT and main OT complex is in final
stage. The operation theatre complex with 33 modular operation theatres (MOTs) is in final stage
of completion and when made operational, the waiting period for surgery would get automatically
reduced.
4.49 The Committee is dismayed to note that 3 major and 2 minor OTs in Trauma OT

55
complex do not have medical gas pipe line at present and the supply of gas is ensured by
using Cylinders. The Committee is of the view that MGPS is a life-supporting element
crucial to life of a patient. The Committee, therefore, recommends to the Ministry to
ensure that all the OTs are equipped with MGPS and make them fully equipped with the
latest equipment without any delay especially in trauma complex so as to cater to the
critical patients.
4.50 Attention of the Committee has also been drawn to the recent media reports which states that
in AIIMS Jodhpur, there is a waiting period of more than a year for diagnostic tests specifically
MRI. The Committee was also informed that waiting time for surgeries varied from Department
to Department. Detail of waiting time is as under:-
Name of Department Waiting Time
Department of Surgery Nil
Department of Onco-surgery Around 1 month
Department of Orthopaedics Around 3 months
Department of ENT Around 6 months
Department of Urology Around 45 days
Department of paediatric surgery Around 6 months
4.51 The Committee is surprised at the long waiting period for basic diagnostic tests and
demands the institute to submit the waiting period for all the diagnostic tests in AIIMS
Jodhpur specifically MRI. The Committee is saddened to note that despite limited functional
OPD to AIIMS standards there is a waiting time for surgeries and even ENT procedure.  If
AIIMS, Jodhpur has to enhance its perception, there has to be minimal waiting time for
surgeries and other procedures.
4.52 The Committee during the course of its study visit to Jodhpur found that not a single
modular OT was functioning. The 9 major OTs and 3 Minor OTs were make shift OTs in
the OPD blocks and not were in consonance with the original drawings submitted by the
DDPRC which had been duly approved. These OT designs were arbitrarily changed on the
pretext that there were designing faults. The Committee has been further apprised that
there was no provision for AHU in the original drawing. The Committee is constrained to
observe that the matter was not taken up with the DDPRC and nor was it penalized or its
contract terminated. The Committee would like to be apprised of the action taken by the
Ministry against the DDPRC and also whether the Ministry had provided for the provision
of Modular OTs in the initial design itself. The Committee is of the opinion that this
negligence has led to severe delays with great financial implications in the form of arbitration
claims submitted by the contractor L&T in this case besides creating structural stability
concerns. The Committee strongly recommends for a thorough probe in the entire matter
including fixing responsibility. The Committee would also like to be apprised whether this
has resulted in any cost/time overruns besides creating grounds for arbitration and submit
the same to the Committee within three months of submission of this report.

56
4.53 The Committee would also like to be apprised of the reasons for time overrun of the
project from the originally planned two years to almost eight years and whether this has
lead to the reported `````150 crore arbitration claims by L&T. The Committee desires that a
report on the arbitration status along with the position taken by the contractor and the
Ministry may also be provided so that the matter can be expeditiously resolved without any
loss to the exchequer.
4.54 The Committee also recommends AIIMS Jodhpur to expedite the construction of OTs
in a time-bound manner and reduce the waiting time period for patients awaiting surgical
procedures thereby reducing their distress and out-of-pocket expenditure.  The Committee
would also like the Institute to submit a quarterly report on the number of procedures done
per OT per day in order to gauge the optimum utilization of the functional OTs.
4.55 The Committee has not been able to assess the number of surgical procedures carried
out in AIIMS Jodhpur due to the variation in the data furnished by the Institute and the
Ministry. The Committee, therefore, strongly recommends that precise data with respect to
major and minor surgeries done (patient-wise and Department-wise) till date since inception
of the Institute may be submitted to the Committee, within three months of presentation
of this Report.
Status of Intensive Care Units (ICUS), Trauma and Emergency Services and Wards
4.56 The Intensive Care Units (Adult, Pediatric and Neonatal) were operationalised from September,
2014. The Committee was apprised that  AIIMS Jodhpur has 3 Intensive Care Units adequately
equipped with the state-of-art medical equipment and machines with 100% occupancy. These
three units are Adult ICU (AICU: 7 beds), Pediatric ICU (PICU: 6 beds) and Neonatal ICU
(NICU: 6 beds) with seven ventilators in AICU and five ventilators in each PICU and NICU with
more than 80% usage of ventilator services. In future, Institute is likely to have 115 state-of-Art
ICU beds.
4.57 T rauma and Emergency Service, which started in October, 2017 is a specialised Department
of AIIMS Jodhpur, with advanced facilities this area. There is separate triage area for Trauma
victims in Trauma and Emergency. All advanced facilities like intubation, resuscitation, ventilators
are available in Emergency. Advanced Cardiac Life Support (ATLS) and Advanced Trauma Life
Support (ACLS) trained doctors are available round the clock in Trauma and Emergency. More than
14,289 patients have been treated in Trauma and Emergency AIIMS Jodhpur since its inception to
September, 2018.
4.58 In response to a query seeking level of preparedness of AIIMS, Jodhpur for huge  number
of casualaties in case of any unfortunate incident, it was informed that with three ICUs having 19
beds, 12 OTs and 684 beds in 20 wards, AIIMS Jodhpur is well equipped to deal with huge
number of casualties in case of any disaster. AIIMS, Jodhpur has a MoU with the Indian Army
for providing services during the times of war and calamities.
4.59 The Committee observes that availability of 17 ventilators against 19 beds would not
cater in case of 100 per cent occupancy at any given time. Even 100 per cent bed occupancy
with mere 19 beds in the ICUs in normal circumstances would not cater to emergency
situations because all units would be fully occupied. The Committee recommends that the

57
number of bed in ICU be increased to accommodate all critically ill patients visiting the
hospital. The Committee also notes that emergency services have not yet started on 24x7x365
days basis and only referred cases are being taken inspite of the fact that the trauma
building was completed in 2014-15. The Committee however appreciates the integrated approach
of the Institute to have a synergy with the Indian army so as to cater to emergency
situations and recommends the institute to start 2 4x7x 365 days trauma/emergency care.
4.60 The Committee has been informed that total number of sanctioned wards in AIIMS, Jodhpur
is 26 while 20 wards are functional at present. Further, out of sanctioned bed strength of 960 only
684 are functional at present.
4.61 The Committee finds the figure of 684 functional beds, 70% of the total sanctioned
bed strength (960) in 20 different wards functional at the Institute. The lower number of
functional beds and even further lower level of bed occupancy reflects poor perception of
healthcare services being provided by the Institute. The Committee is concerned that existing
beds are insufficient to cater to the growing health needs of the people. Such inadequacies
have forced people across socio-economic strata to unaffordable private healthcare facilities.
The Institute needs to urgently increase the bed occupancy by improving its perception to
address the health services for the people of the region.
Burns and Plastic Surgery Department
4.62 AIIMS, Jodhpur has a separate Burns and Plastic Surgery Department where the patients are
treated on OPD/IPD basis. However, there is no separate Burn unit as the provision was not made
in the original project of AIIMS, Jodhpur. Patient with serious burn injury require separate isolated
ward for they are prone to infections, and cannot be kept in the ward along with the other patients.
4.63 The Committee is of the view that given the high incidences of burn injuries in the
State of Rajasthan and adjoining areas, a separate Burn Unit at the Institute is the need
of the hour. This will ensure that timely and adequate treatment to burn patients in terms
of reducing their mortality, complications and rehabilitation. The Committee, thus, strongly
recommends that the Institute must start a separate Burns Unit in IPD with sufficient
manpower and necessary equipment to improve the care and facilities for such patients at
the earliest. The Committee, in this connection, recommends that ambulance services equipped
with advanced features for transportation of patient from accident site to the burns unit of
the Institute should be made available.
AYUSH Facilities
4.64 As regards, AYUSH facility at AIIMS, Jodhpur, the Committee was informed that AYUSH
services started at AIIMS, Jodhpur on 6th November, 2017 with the concept of holistic healthcare.
Since the inception of AYUSH services, the Institute has treated 4376 OPD patients. However, the
Institute is not providing IPD services in AYUSH.
4.65 AIIMS Jodhpur has one AYUSH Medical Officer against sanctioned strength of five and one
Yoga Instructor. At present 2 Medical Officers are working in AYUSH Department on contractual
basis. They were appointed for tenure of 11 months initially and their tenure was extended further
for a period of one year. AYUSH also has Homoeopathic pharmacy and Swarnaprashan Sanskar.

58
The Committee also notes that the construction of AYUSH building was completed in 2014-15 but
even today the 30 beds in AYUSH Department are lying vacant. The Committee also observes that
Panchkarma facility is not yet started and the required human resource is not available at the
AYUSH Department.
4.66 The Committee observes that only Ayurveda and Homoeopathic OPD services are functional
at the AIIMS, Jodhpur.  The Committee considers that there is a need to integrate the
Ayurveda, Y oga, Naturopathy, Unani, Siddha, and Homeopathy (AYUSH) system of indigenous
medicine to meet the shortage of health care professionals and strengthen the delivery
system of the health care service through an alternate system of Indian medicine. The
Committee, therefore, recommends to the Ministry to start other AYUSH services at the
Institute. The Committee also recommends to the Ministry to ensure the accessibility and
availability and choice of AYUSH services to all patients depending in these specific needs.
The Committee recommends to the institute to chalk out strategy for strengthening AYUSH
services and to support this system with adequate infrastructure and manpower in a time
bound manner.
4.67 The Committee is constrained to observe that out of seven sanctioned posts for AYUSH
faculty, only two faculties are presently working at AIIMS, Jodhpur and that too on contract
basis. The Committee observes that posts of Senior Medical Officer AYUSH and Y oga instructor
are lying vacant. The Committee, thus, recommends the Ministry to fill up the posts for
AYUSH doctors on regular  basis at the earliest.
Governing Body
4.68 The Committee during its deliberations and visit to AIIMS, Jodhpur found that the
meetings of the Governing Body at the Institute were not being held on a regular basis.
The Members of the Governing Body are not consulted while taking decisions during the
meeting and all the decisions are taken by few incumbents. The Committee is of the firm
view that the meetings of the Governing Body at the Institute should be conducted on time
and participation of all members of the body should be ensured while taking important
decisions.  All AIIMS like institutions should strive to conduct regular meetings and participation
of all members be encouraged while taking important decisions of the Institute. The Committee,
accordingly, recommends that AIIMS, Jodhpur must give due importance to all the Members
of the Governing Body while taking any important decision to make it democratically
decentralized.  All out efforts must be made for strengthening of Governing Body and other
Committees for better functioning of AIIMS, Jodhpur.
4.69 The Committee is of the considered opinion that autonomy and accountability need to
be balanced. The Committee welcomes the appointment of the Presidents of the Institute
and the constitution of the CIB at the Central level. This will ensure a sense of responsibility
in the decision making process.

CHAPTER-V
AIIMS, PATNA
5.1 AIIMS Patna was established under the “Setting up of AIIMS like Institutions” component of
PMSSY to address the need of tertiary healthcare services and provide specialised quality care to
the citizens. AIIMS Patna was inaugurated on 25th September, 2012 on the lines of AIIMS, Delhi
to bridge the existing gap in advance medical investigation and affordable treatment and also serve
as an Institute of quality medical education in the State.
5.2 As per information submitted by AIIMS Patna, there is an average daily OPD footfall of 1775
in the Institute and out of the sanctioned 960 beds, only 643 beds are functional. The Doctor:
Patient ratio at the Institute is 1:5 whereas the Bed: Patient ratio is 1:0.75.
Budgetary Allocation
5.3 In response to the Committee’s query about the total budgetary allocations given to AIIMS
Patna, the Institute submitted the following figures for the period 2011-18:-

5.4 The Committee expresses its deep concern that the percentage of underutilisation with
respect to total Grants-in-Aid of AIIMS, Patna has been 64.72% in 2012-13, 37.98% in
2013-14, 47.15% in 2014-15, 32.33% in 2015-16, 42.86% in 2016-17 and 62.61% in 2017-18.
The closing balance was as high as ````` 195.95 crore for the year 2015-16 and 208.70 crore for
the year 2016-17 that express low absorption capacity of implementing agencies. The Committee
also observes that in the year 2014-15, the percentage of utilization under GiA (capital) was
18.73% which further decreased to 12.93% in 2015-16. In 2016-17, the opening balance
under capital grants in aids was 140.73 crore implying that out of the total 161.63 crore
allotted under GiA (Capital) only Rs 20.90 crore were spent in capital formation in 2015-
16. All these date speak volume of poor financial planning estimation and control execution
of construction scheme.
5.5 The Committee laments the fact that AIIMS Patna that is envisaged as an apex
institute of medical learning and affordable tertiary health care has failed miserably in

61
optimum utilisation of its budget. The budgetary expenditure pattern of AIIMS Patna exposes
the lackadaisical attitude of the Institute and brings to the foreground the Institute’s
incompetency to use the financial resources to its fullest potential. This gross underutilisation
of resources is a grave matter of concern especially when AIIMS Patna lags behind on many
fronts and struggles to provide quality healthcare to the citizens.
5.6 CAG report also highlights that funds to the tune of ` 496.95 crore were made available to
AIIMS Patna during 2011-17, out of which the expenditure incurred by the Institute was ` 338.03
crore leaving an unspent balance of ` 158.92 crore and 31.98 % of unutilised fund. CAG has also
pointed out that the Ministry was not compiling or monitoring expenditure being incurred by new
AIIMS/agencies against releases made by it. Thus, no mechanism was in place for monitoring and
tracking actual expenditure leading to accumulation of unspent funds with the new AIIMS and the
agencies.
5.7 The Committee taking cognizance of CAG’s view on unutilised funds by AIIMS, Patna
holds the Ministry equally responsible for the utter idling of funds and increase in capital
costs. The Committee, in this connection, recommends that the Ministry should immediately
develop a mechanism for monitoring the budget estimates and ensure utilisation of funds
by AIIMS Patna and other new AIIMS so that obstacles and hindrances, if any, are identified
at an early stage and the cost and time overrun are avoided, while implementing the project
under PMSSY.
STATUS OF CONSTRUCTION WORK
5.8 When inquired about the status of construction activities in AIIMS Patna, it submitted that
the construction of Medical College and Residential Complex is complete. The work is 95%, 99%
and 83.5 % complete in hospital complex, electrical services and estate services respectively as
evident in the table below:-

5.9 The Committee deprecates the slow pace of construction activities at AIIMS Patna. As
per the information given by AIIMS Patna, the construction of hospital complex under
Package II was originally deemed to be completed by 10th September, 2012 but even after
6 years, the project is incomplete. Under package III and IV , the work of electrical services
and Estate services were to be completed by December, 2012 and February, 2013 respectively
but it is yet to be completed.  The Committee wills to attribute the delay in execution of
construction projects to mismanagement on the part of AIIMS, Patna.
5.10 It is a proven fact that delay in any construction activity leads to unnecessary additional
costs and time extensions. Inflation, abrupt ending of contracts, arbitration cases, disputes
etc further increases the cost of any delayed project. Systemic analysis at the inception of
any project and regular monitoring can easily control all these factors and minimize the
chances of delay. Sadly, the Ministry and the Institute remained ignorant of both time and
cost deadlines for the completion of the project as envisaged for AIIMS Patna.
5.11 In response to reasons w.r.t. the delay of construction activities, AIIMS Patna has submitted
that professional expertise in constructing hospital building is still in the stage of infancy in India.
There is very little research work available in terms of material and design to suit the local
conditions. AIIMS Patna cited the following reasons for the delay in construction work:-
(i) Mismatch in drawing and bill of quantity which led to deviation in the quantity needing
timely decision.
(ii) Midway change in project consultant.
(iii) No Program Evaluation Review Technique (PERT) Chart.
(iv) Items were procured which were not needed at that point of time.
   1 2 3 4 5678 9

63
(v) Engineering procurement system should have been followed rather that involving multiple
contractor for same project to avoid mismatch in time line.
(vi) Siphoning of money by Contractor from one project to another project.
5.12 The Committee observes with anguish that AIIMS Patna has failed to realize the
implications of this sluggish rate of construction activities and is of the view that the
reasons cited by AIIMS Patna for the construction delays are inexcusable and could have
been easily avoided had the Institute been alert and committed for the Project. Moreover,
the argument of AIIMS Patna that professional expertise in constructing hospital building
is missing in the country is not tenable and bereft of fact and logic.
5.13 The Committee is dismayed to note that no project management tool/strategy has been
employed for construction of an AIIMS like institution that is intended to be the beacon of
light for the tertiary healthcare sector in the State. A PERT or Program Evaluation Review
Technique Chart is one such project management tool that helps in better analysis of the
resources and the timeline of any project.  In the absence of any evaluation technique, the
Committee is really skeptical about the completion of the construction work under the
remaining packages. The Committee recommends that a special project monitoring system
for AIIMS Patna must be devised to track the progress of construction activities undertaken
by the Institute so as to ensure better project management. The Committee strongly recommends
that the work under Package II, III and IV must be completed on time with no further
delays.
5.14 CAG in its Report of 2018 has also noted that in AIIMS-Patna, there is a deviation of 158%
to 173% in construction of medical college building and Hospital Complex {Package I and Package
II) as compared to quantity in the Bill of Quantities (BOQ) of the contract and ` 41.68 crore were
incurred due to deviation.
5.15 AIIMS Patna has informed that quantity of steel per cubic meter of Reinforced Cement
Concrete (RCC) was taken as 102.92/cum which is too less in BOQ, whereas quantity of steel/
cum of RCC is 172Kg/cum in the GFC drawing provided by DDPR. The construction deviation
due to difference in scope of work in the architectural and structural drawing like double outer
wall and shading to window as required for Green Building, external cantilever RCC slab (at
roof level)/ Chhajja (at window cill & lintel level) has had an effect on the quantity of steel
reinforcement.
5.16 The Committee is at pains to take into account the improper estimation of quantities
of items pointed out by CAG in its report. Surprisingly, the deviation is upto 173% in case
of AIIMS Patna which clearly indicates that the requirement of different items had been
under-assessed and unrealistic assessment of the site conditions were made. The green
building norms and the Energy Conservation Building Code (ECBC) should have been
implemented before the beginning of the construction activities. The Committee is highly
distressed at the inadequate technical estimation made before the construction of AIIMS
Patna.
5.17 The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Ministry should make a detailed
technical and realistic assessment before setting up of other new AIIMS under PMSSY by

64
carrying out proper field and site surveys and the errors while constructing AIIMS Patna
should not be repeated.
Expansion Program of AIIMS Patna
5.18 AIIMS Patna has submitted that there is a requirement of funds to the tune of ` 250 crores
from Central Government for purchase of land and construction of administrative, academic,
residential building and transit facility for patients’ attendants. The institute has also submitted that
the present infrastructure of the institute is not sufficient to accommodate all the Departments. The
expansion program of the Institute includes building Dental College, PG Lecture Theatre, academic
buildings to house all the Departments. AIIMS, Patna submitted the requirements of fund for the
construction of the following:
(i) ` 100 crores - Residential Building
(ii) ` 15 crores - Administrative Block
(iii) ` 115 crores - Academic Building
(iv) ` 20 crores - Transit Facility
5.19 The Institute has also requested the Ministry of Health & Family Welfare to sanction `100
crore for construction of Residential Complex - Phase-II which is yet to be approved by the
Ministry.  The institute also submitted that purchasing of land from the State Government is critical
for the expansion programme and it has already requested the State Government for the piece of
land near the present campus for its expansion program. AIIMS Patna cited fund and land un-
availability as major challenges in its expansion program. The Institute has planned to take up this
issue in its Standing Finance Committee meeting (SFC).
5.20 The Committee while scrutinizing the expenditure pattern of funds in AIIMS Patna
finds that between 2013 and 2018, the Institute had unspent balance under Grants-in-Aid
Capital Head. The Committee understands that construction activities require substantial
amount of funds and approval in advance, but at the same time the Committee cannot
ignore the poor track record of the Institute wherein inordinate delays have caused huge
time and cost overrun under different packages of construction activities.  The Committee
is of the firm view that the demand for funds should be realistic and should also commensurate
with the trend of utilization. The Committee, therefore, recommends that the Ministry of
Health and Family Welfare must evaluate the demand of AIIMS Patna on realistic grounds
and forward the same to the Ministry of Finance for consideration.
5.21 The Committee understands that land is critical for the expansion program of the
institute. Therefore, the Committee urges upon the Ministry to take up the matter with the
State Government to provide the required amount of land to the Institute. The Committee
while underlining the need for allocation of required funds to AIIMS Patna for infrastructure
construction also advises the Ministry to hold regular review meetings to monitor the new
construction projects so that the mistakes committed in the first phase of construction
activities are not repeated at all costs. The Ministry should also carry out regular inspections
and seek monthly report from the Institute on the status and progress of the projects.

65
Procurement of Medical Equipment
5.22 AIIMS Patna informed the Committee that a budget of ` 200 crore was allocated to AIIMS
Patna for procurement of both pre-clinical and clinical medical equipment. Out of the allocated
budget, both HLL (acting as Procurement Support Agency) and individual AIIMS under minimum
operational requirement had to procure equipment as per the approved list.
5.23 AIIMS Patna in its written submission informed that they follow the procedure as laid down
in the GFR 2017 and Manual for procurement of Materials and services issued by Ministry of
Finance. The Institute uploads all its tenders in the Central Public Procurement Portal (CPP) and
utilizes Government e-Marketplace (GeM) for materials, which are available in it.
5.24 In reply to a specific query regarding the delay in supply, installation and non-utilization of
the m edical equipment in AIIMS Patna, it submitted that due to non-availability of site-like bunker,
complete power and delay in obtaining site approval as per AERB norms, high value items
like High energy LINAC, HDR Brachytherapy, CT Simulator, Gamma Camera etc. could not be
delivered.
5.25 AIIMS Patna has submitted that HLL has placed orders valuing ` 7.527 crore for Pre-clinical
Departments and ` 159.49 for Clinical Departments till 8th November, 2018. The total procurement
carried out for AIIMS Patna comprising equipment for pre-clinical and clinical Departments is
` 172.94 crore. Hence, the budget left out for procurement is ` 27.063 crore.

5.27 The Committee is dismayed to note that 16 items of equipment (8.33%) costing ````` 56
crore remain undelivered while 34 items of equipment (19.54 %) costing ````` 34 crore have
been delivered but not installed. Consignee Receipt Certificate (CRC) has not been issued
for 20 items of equipment (11.49%) costing ````` 3 crore. The Committee is shocked over the
state of affairs in this institute of National importance where such grave underutilization
of resources has become a common phenomenon. The Committee cannot fathom that how
can the Institute turn a blind eye to the number of medical equipment that are lying
uninstalled in the hospitals for such long periods.
5.28 The Committee is not at all convinced by the reasons given by AIIMS Patna for the
delayed supplies and non-installation of the medical equipment. Delays due to the non-
availability of space or complete power etc. could have been easily addressed by the Institute.
The Committee believes that the institute had plenty of time at its disposal to obtain site
approvals and the site should have been prepared well in advance. This undue delay in
installation also highlights the failure of the Procurement Support Agency in ensuring
timely supply and installation of the medical equipment in the Institute. The Committee is
also highly concerned regarding the role of the Procurement Support Agency that should
have analyzed the area and power requirement for the medical equipment and communicated
the same to the Institute. The Committee holds the Institute and the Procurement Support
Agency responsible for the delay in operationalization of these equipment and also warns
that this lax attitude of the institute has proved to be the biggest roadblock in its path to
establish itself as a centre of excellence in medical education.
5.29 The Committee, therefore strongly recommends that the institute should be proactive
in taking suo moto  cognizance of the delay in delivery of the equipment and ensure that
medical equipment are delivered on time. The Institute should also make sure that unnecessary
delay in installation of equipment does not take place due to shortage of skilled manpower
or lack in infrastructure. The Committee also recommends that all the medical equipment
should be accepted only after a technical inspection by a technical Inspector and should
receive routine maintenance and calibration. The Institute should also have a contingency
plan in case of breakdown of equipment so that the diagnostic services are not adversely
effected.

medical equipment in AIIMS Patna (Table 1 and 2).

5.32 When inquired about the same, AIIMS Patna in its reply submitted that in spite of generic
specifications, widespread advertisement in national dailies and CPPP portal the numbers of bidders
are less resulting in single bid or no-bid in several cases.
5.33 The Committee fails to understand that why even simple items such as museum jar
and LED TV were procured through single bid. The Committee notes that multiple bids were
received in these two cases but were rejected on the basis of technical specifications. The
Committee strongly feels that technical requirements should not be misused as a means to
limit the number of bids or minimize competition as is evident by the data submitted by
AIIMS Patna. The Committee recommends that the institute should clearly specify the
minimum technical and financial requirements ensuring an equal and fair chance to all the
bidders.
5.34 The Committee also observes that the price at which the equipment were procured
may not have been the best price as the Institute failed to hold a competitive procurement
process. The Committee also desires to be apprised of the number of bidders, the reasons
for the rejection of their proposal and the compliance proposal of the winning bidder in each
case for procurement of museum Jar, LED TV, Co2 Laser, Carl Zesis OPMI Vario and
Computerised Virtual Dissection Table. The Committee desires AIIMS Patna to submit a
written technical evaluation summary for each case of proprietary/single bid procurement
till date to it within three months from the presentation of this Report. The Committee also
recommends that the Director of the Institute should be made accountable for all cases of
single bid/proprietary procurement wherever irregularities are reported/observed in future.
5.35 AIIMS Patna also submitted that US FDA and CE certification was put in the specification
due to unavailability of a similar Indian Model.  The Committee is shocked by the false
claims of AIIMS Patna that Indian model for basic instruments such as hand drill, mouth
gags, rib, hooks, chisels etc. are not available in the market. The Committee is of the
opinion that items such as Navigation system, Surgical Helmet and Accessories, Surgical
consumable items and other instruments are easily available with the Indian manufacturers
but AIIMS Patna, by insisting on US FDA and CE certification even for such basic instruments,
has clearly shown contempt for the Indian manufacturing sector.

70
5.36 On one side where the Government is taking measures to reduce the country’s dependency
on imported medical devices, it is unfortunate to find an institute of national importance
openly following a mandatory US FDA certification policy for purchasing medical equipment.
The Committee is distressed to note that medical devices like defibrillator, ultrasound
machine, neonatal ventilator, surgical instruments etc. are being purchased with US FDA
certification even though there are many other manufacturers with CE certification. The
Committee strongly recommends to AIIMS Patna to follow a buy “Make in India” policy and
insist on Bureau of Indian Standard (BIS) certification.
5.37 The Committee is shocked at the audacity of the Institute by its claims to have
purchased beds with cushions, equipment for PMR Department, two battery operated hand
drill accessories etc. with US FDA certification. As per the information submitted by AIIMS
Patna, it procured 250 fowler beds with cushion at a price of ````` 1.1 crore with an extra
Comprehensive Maintenance Contract (CMC) price of ````` 24 lakhs, two battery operated hand
drill accessories at ````` 46 lakhs with CMC price of 7.9 lakhs, equipment for PMR Department
at ````` 14 lakhs with CMC price of ````` 4.6 lakh. This superfluous spending by AIIMS Patna
raises serious concern over the lack of monitoring of the procurement Policy. The Committee
recommends for an enquiry into the procurement of fowler beds with cushions as these are
easily available with Indian manufacturers at a much lower price paid by AIIMS, Patna. The
Committee senses large irregularities in the procurement process of the Institute and seeks
a detailed probe of all the equipment that have been procured in the last two years. The
Committee desires AIIMS Patna to submit a detailed report on the same, within three
months of the presentation of this report to the Parliament.

for Endocrinology, Nephrology, Urology, Nuclear Medicine and Hospital Management. The numbers
of available faculty in AIIMS Patna against the sanctioned strength has been given at Annexure-
A. Some of the Departments have shortage of Faculty/Doctors and hence are not fully functional
as per the details given below:-

5.39 The Committee notes that 122 posts have been filled up against the sanctioned strength
of 305 for Professors/Additional Professor/Associate Professor/Assistant Professor whereas
183 Posts are lying vacant in different Departments of AIIMS Patna. None of the posts have
been filled up in Trauma and Emergency, Nuclear Medicine, Nephrology, Hospital Administration
and Endocrinology and Metabolism Departments. The Committee observes with concern that
important Departments such as Cardiology, Medical Oncology and Haematology, Neonatology,
Neurology, Radiotherapy, Surgical Oncology, Urology are functioning with only one doctor in
AIIMS Patna. The Committee also notes with serious concern that AIIMS Patna has the
lowest faculty strength among all the new AIIMS established under Phase 1 of PMSSY. This
casts aspersion on the functioning of AIIMS, Patna with regard to Human Resource Management
as well.
5.40 W ith regards to the inadequate recruitment of faculty staff, AIIMS Patna in its written reply
submitted that the recruitment of human resource in the Institute has been given high priority.
Attention of the Committee has been drawn to various initiatives by the Ministry to tide over the
problem of non-availability of suitable candidates for faculty positions in various speciality and
super-speciality disciplines which are as follows:-
(i) Provision has been made for engaging retired faculty from premier Institutes such as
AIIMS-Delhi, PGI-Chandigarh, JIPMER-Puducherry and others, up to the age of 70
years.
(ii) Provisions have also been made for recruiting faculty from other Government medical
colleges/Institutes on deputation basis.
(iii) Power of Directors in the matter of appointment of faculty and non-faculty has also
have been announced.
(iv) System of campus recruitment has been introduced to tap the bright young passing out
students from AIIMS-Delhi and other premier Institutes.
(v) Approval of Ministry of Home Affairs has been obtained for allowing PIO card holders
to be appointed as regular faculty in new AIIMS.

72
(vi) The new AIIMS have been empowered to invite visiting faculty from premier Institutes
in India and abroad for a period of two years.
5.41 The Committee is disturbed by the acute shortage of Professors/Additional Professor/
Associate Professor/Assistant Professor in AIIMS Patna and other AIIMS like Institutes.
The Committee during its study visit to AIIMS Patna also discussed the shortage of facility
with the Director and representatives of the Ministry. The Committee recommends that
AIIMS Patna should make immediate efforts to implement all the initiatives for recruitment
of faculty. The Committee notes that provision has been made to engage faculty from other
institutes and Government medical colleges on deputation but the Committee would also
like to emphasize that engaging faculty from the GMCs should not result in vacancies in
the nearby GMCs.
5.42 The Committee understands that special packages/incentives or higher salary may not
always be a driving factor to serve in this part of the country, therefore, the Institute has
to explore alternative means such as paid research collaboration, high end technology,
better working hours, security,  enough leisure time etc.  to attract qualified doctors to the
Institute.

5.43 As per the information submitted by AIIMS Patna, out of the total sanctioned strength of
3776 in the non-faculty position, 1431 (including outsourced and contracted posts) have been filled
while 2345 posts are still lying vacant. Posts were advertised for Group B and Group C on regular
basis in the year 2013 and 2014. After that Recruitment Rule was changed by the Ministry in the
year 2015 and the same was approved by GB/IB in September, 2016. There was no regular
Director from November, 2016 to February, 2017 and new Director joined on 28.02.2017. Tender
for Computer Based Test (CBT) was floated in November 2017 and final agreement was signed
with TCS as successful bidder in September, 2018. Advertisement for recruitment will be issued
soon.

5.44 The Committee understands that the shortage of doctors and specialist is a macro level
problem considering the poor doctor: patient ratio in the country but the Committee fails
to understand the reasons for huge number of vacancy in the non-faculty positions at
AIIMS Patna. The Committee strongly recommends that the Institute should expedite the
recruitment process for the non-faculty posts.
5.45 The Committee also feels that AIIMS Patna should not ignore the importance of
nurses in the health care system and recommends expediting the recruitment of 500 staff
nurses for which AIIMS Patna has already issued advertisement. The institute should also
speed up its efforts to start different medical and para-medical courses to fill the existing
gaps in their medical services.
5.46 The Committee feels that this huge shortage against faculty and non-faculty posts in
AIIMS Patna has an adverse effect on the functioning of many important Departments in
the Institute impacting the overall quality of medical service provided to the patients.
Therefore, the Committee recommends a common centralised recruitment policy for filling
up the vacancies in all the AIIMS. This will not only lower the cost of recruitment process
but also offer a uniform and fair selection criteria for all the AIIMS.

5.48 The Committee has been informed that Endocrinology, Nephrology, Urology, Nuclear Medicine
and Hospital Management are not functional in AIIMS Patna. As per data submitted by it, presently
Sl. No Department Sanctioned Working V acant

75
23 Speciality and 13 Super Speciality Departments are functional in the Institute. The Committee
observes that many posts are vacant in important Speciality Departments such as Anaesthesiology,
Anatomy, and General Medicine etc. Though 13 Super speciality Departments are functional in
AIIMS Patna but the large number of vacancy in each of these Departments is a grave concern
and raises serious question over the quality of healthcare.
5.49 In reply to a query about offering of incentives to the specialists and super specialists, AIIMS
Patna submitted that the decision needs to be taken by the Ministry of Health and Family Welfare.
5.50 The Committee believes that Speciality and Super Speciality Departments are fundamental
for an Institute like AIIMS Patna that is envisaged as a flag bearer in providing tertiary
health care and medical education. The Committee however is anguished to note that
important Departments like Endocrinology, Nephrology, Urology, and Nuclear Medicine are
not functional till date in the Institute. Considering the fact that around 2.2 lakh new
patients suffering from End Stage Renal Disease (ESRD) are reported every year which
results in an additional demand of 3.4 crore dialysis every year, the status of crucial
Department like Nephrology being non-functional in AIIMS Patna is a matter of grave
concern.
5.51 The Institute needs to be considerate towards the expenses borne by poor patients
when they are referred to other hospitals because of non-availability of the medical facility
in AIIMS Patna. The Committee, thus, recommends to the Institute to start the Specialty
and Super Speciality Departments as early as possible in the best interest of the people of
the State.
5.52 The Committee recognises the fact that there is a dire need to address the shortage
of specialist and start the recruitment process for each Department. AIIMS Patna’s contention
that the decision for offering incentives to Specialists/Super Specialist will be taken by the
Ministry amounts to shirking off its responsibilities and therefore, the Committee strongly
recommends that the proposal of offering special incentives and packages to Specialist and
Super Specialists in AIIMS Patna be submitted to the Ministry for consideration without
much delay.
Medical Education
5.53 AIIMS Patna in its submission before the Committee stated that there are total 39 Departments
functional in AIIMS Patna, with 100 MBBS seats and 60 seats in nursing courses. Presently, the
Institute has MBBS and Nursing courses and is planning to start courses in para-medical, technician
etc. AIIMS Patna also provides facility of General Medicine Care, and super speciality treatment
for the people of Bihar. The average per day OPD and IPD footfall is 1775 and 49 respectively
(as per September, 2018).
5.54 The Committee was also informed that AIIMS Patna is actively involved in research activities
and post graduate courses with 78 students will start from January, 2019. Other Departments are
being evaluated by the External Experts from AIIMS, New Delhi and soon these Departments will
start PG/DM/M.ch process from July 2019 session. AIIMS Patna has 10 crores of extramural
funding in research and 28 extramural projects are being funded by agencies such as ICMR,
UNICEF, DBT, DST, WHO and Government of Bihar.

76
5.55 The Committee is unhappy to note the slow progress in starting Post Graduate courses
in different Departments of the Institute. The Institute should take cognizance of the acute
shortage of specialists in the country in general and in Bihar in particular and therefore
need to focus on producing better qualified specialists. The Committee, at the same time,
is aggrieved at the insufficient number of faculty staff in AIIMS Patna and worries that in
the absence of the required number of Professors/Additional Professor/Associate Professor/
Assistant Professor in the Institute, the quality of medical education may suffer. Therefore,
the Committee recommends that AIIMS Patna, before starting any Post graduate course in
the Institute, should ensure that the necessary infrastructure and human resource is available
with the Institute.
5.56 The Committee reprimands that AIIMS Patna does not have a human resource development
cell for undertaking regular induction and in service training programs for capacity building
of the staff. The Committee believes that training of personnel is critical for development
of high standards of medical education in AIIMS Patna. The Committee, therefore, strongly
recommends creation of a Human Resource Development Cell in AIIMS Patna that would
conduct regular training for the doctors, nurses and the staff. The Institute must also
conduct special training for ensuring palliative care to the patients.
5.57 As regards clinical training, AIIMS Patna stated that in the beginning of new session every
year, it conducts orientation programme and briefs the students about theory and clinical learning
details. There is one senior resident per 3 MBBS student. Approximately 20 students are posted per
ward as per clinical posting schedule. Clinical training is done as per each Departmental pre
specified standard and is imparted under faculty and senior residents.
5.58 The Committee is sceptical about the standard of clinical training provided as the
Institute has shortage of faculty in almost every Department. The Committee is anguished
to find that there are insufficient number of Operation Theatres and laboratories as the
construction work is yet to be completed. The Committee strongly emphasizes that the
assessment of the budding doctors should be based on real clinical cases rather than on
rote learning. Ideally, each hospital should have senior consultants to advise the resident
doctors on treatment or supervise the key procedure and surgeries but the shortage of
trained specialists in AIIMS Patna may produce graduates and post graduates with no
practical medical skills. Therefore, the Committee lays more impetus on imparting practical,
technical and skill based knowledge to the doctors. The Committee is of the view that
AIIMS Patna should, therefore, focus on producing better trained doctors who later become
competent enough to work in a clinical setting and possess sharp clinical and medical
acumen.
Operation Theatres
5.59 The AIIMS Director in his submission before the Committee submitted that while 28 Operation
Theatres (OTs) have been sanctioned in AIIMS Patna but at present only 13 are functional, out
of which 11 are Modular Operation Theatres. As per information given by AIIMS Patna, 3174
major and minor surgeries and 506 emergency surgeries (including Caesarean), have been performed
till 25 November, 2018. The waiting time in surgical procedures varies from 1 week to 6 months

77
due to the limited availability of OTs at present. AIIMS Patna also informed that more OTs will
be made functional by procuring equipment and recruiting manpower so that the waiting time of
surgeries is reduced.
5.60 From the data submitted by AIIMS Patna, the Committee observes with deep concern
that AIIMS, Patna has conducted on an average 10 surgeries per day in its 13 Operation
Theatres. Moreover, only 506 emergency surgeries have been conducted. The Committee
finds that these 13 OTs remain highly underutilised although AIIMS Patna cited limited
availability of OTs as the reason behind the waiting time in surgical procedures. The Committee
also recommends to AIIMS, Patna for planning higher number of surgeries to be carried out
in a day so that one OT can be used for more than one surgery in a day, thus, resulting
in optimum utilisation of the Operation Theatre.
5.61 As per the information submitted by the Ministry, AIIMS Patna has conducted 4224
major and minor surgeries till September, 2018, the Committee is displeased with such huge
variation in the data and would like AIIMS Patna to submit precise data with respect to
surgical procedures (patient-wise and Department-wise) till date, since inception of the Institute,
within three months of presentation of this Report.
Trauma and Emergency Services
5.62 AIIMS Patna in its written submission has informed that the Trauma/Emergency service has
already started in the Institute with total 103 beds including 9 ICU + 8 HDU beds Patna to take
up serious patients. However, the Committee has been given to understand that the available faculty
against the sanctioned strength for Trauma and Emergency services is as given below:-
(As on 10.12.18)
Department Sanctioned W orking V acant
Trauma and Emergency 13 0 13
5.63 The Committee takes it to exception that all the faculty positions in the Trauma and
Emergency Care Centre are vacant. Considering this fact, AIIMS Patna’s claim that it has
a functional trauma/emergency centre is absolutely farce. The Committee reprimands the
Institute for making such false claims and misleading the Committee. It is unfortunate that
despite the required infrastructure for Trauma and Emergency centre, it is not fully functional
due to the shortage of skilled manpower. The Committee strongly recommends that recruitment
to various posts is carried out and trauma and Emergency Centre is made functional in the
real sense of the term with adequate manpower so that critical patients are given urgent
and prompt treatment and care.  The Committee takes serious note of the manner in which
AIIMS Patna has tried to evade Parliamentary scrutiny.
Availability of Beds at AIIMS Patna
5.64 AIIMS Patna has informed the Committee that the total number of beds in AIIMS Patna is
643 against the sanctioned bed strength of 960. The number of functional beds will increase further
after completion of remaining two Blocks i.e. B and A of the hospital. The average bed occupancy
rate is 69% in AIIMS Patna. The distribution of beds in each ward is given at Annexure B .

78
5.65 The Committee observes with deep concern that the numbers of beds in some important
Departments are very low. The low bed strength in each Department also reflects the
Institute’s lack of preparedness in case of an unforeseen disaster/natural calamity. Even the
distribution of beds for various Departments appears faulty. The Committee is surprised to
observe that Urology Department has 5 beds but the Department is not yet functional in
the Institute. This again highlights the Institute’s failure to make optimum utilisation of
the available resources. The Committee also notes that the number of patients in Ayush
Department is comparatively low when compared to other Departments. The Committee
therefore is of the opinion that the beds under Ayush Department can be used for other
Departments on a need basis. The Committee strongly recommends that AIIMS Patna
should carry out a detailed assessment of the patient load in different Departments and
accordingly carry out the distribution of beds in each Department. This will certainly help
in better management of the wards and facilitate better bed occupancy in each Department.
The Committee recommends that the construction activities should be expedited so that the
sanctioned bed strength of 960 is achieved in the Institute at the earliest.
Other Infrastructure Development
5.66 The Committee has also noted the following facts about other infrastructural development in
AIIMS Patna:-
1. Mortuary services have not been started. Mortuary Building is ready but installation of
centralised AC is going on and procurement of necessary equipment is under process.
2. Burns and Plastic surgery Department was envisaged in the plan of AIIMS Patna.
However, infrastructure for the purposes of services to the burns patient was not
planned in the DPR. AIIMS, Patna approached the NTPC India to create separate Burn
building having 50 beds, 15 ICU beds, 2 Operation Theatres along with skin bank under
corporate social responsibility. MoU has already been signed and the facility is likely to
become operational within 18 months.
5.67 The Committee is surprised to note that minor issues like non installation of centralized
AC and procurement of equipment has stalled the progress of mortuary service in AIIMS
Patna which is critical for preservation and forensic/pathological investigation of the dead
body. AIIMS Patna, therefore, must accelerate the remaining work in the Mortuary building
and immediately place the proposal for inspection of the building. All equipment must be
procured and installed in time so that Mortuary Service is provided in time. AIIMS Patna
should also ensure that the timeline for completion of Burns and Plastic surgery Department
is met and unwarranted delays do not hamper the functioning of the Department.
Medical Imaging Modalities
5.68 AIIMS Patna submitted the following data with respect to the various radio imaging modalities:-
2016-17 2017-till 25 November, 2018
X-Rays 8,838 23,576
Ultrasonography 4,655 8,019

79
Mammography 177 318

5.69 AIIMS Patna submitted that there is no waiting time for investigation of cases (X ray,
Ultrasound, CT scan, MRI and DSA) for admitted cases (IPD Cases) of the Institute as it is done
within 24 hours. For OPD cases, the average waiting period for X ray is 1 day to 3 days, for
ultrasound routine OPD cases the average waiting period is 3 months, for CT scan the average
waiting period 1 month. For MRI OPD cases the waiting period is 8 Months. AIIMS Patna cited
shortage of MRI machines as the reason for long waiting period and insisted on procurement of
two more MRI machines. AIIMS Patna also submitted that on an average 5 MRIs, 2 X-ray
mammography and 8-10 Sono-mammography are conducted per working day.
5.70 The Committee is shocked at the long waiting period for Ultrasound, CT scan, and
MRI OPD Cases. The Committee during its study visit to Patna also came across patients
with waiting period as long as one year. The medical imaging facilities are fundamental for
the correct diagnosis of any disease but such long waiting period render the whole medical
care process futile and life threatening. The Committee is not at all convinced by the plea
of AIIMS Patna’s management that shortage of MRI machines led to such long waiting
period for MRI scans. The Committee also notes that AIIMS Patna on an average conducted
only 5 MRI per day, 2 Mammography and 18 CT scan which is very low when compared to
the available infrastructure and manpower. The Committee believes that even with one MRI
machine at its disposal, the Institute could have easily carried out more than twenty scans
in one day as is being done in other AIIMS and private hospitals. The grim data furnished
by AIIMS Patna clearly reflects gross underutilization of the available resources and sheds
light on the failure of the institute to work in its full capacity. The Committee during its
earlier study visit observed that RIMS Imphal conducted 30 MRI scans per day on a very
old machine and also carried out over 50 CT scans. The Committee desires a quarterly
update from AIIMS Patna on the per day usage of MRI machine along with the daily
pending cases.  The Committee strongly recommends that the procurement of second MRI
machine should only be done when the present MRI machine is optimally utilised.
Ayush
5.71 The Committee has been informed that only Homoeopathy Department is functional at AIIMS
Patna and services are provided through O.P .D basis. Only 2 Medical Officers have been appointed
against the sanctioned strength of 5 whereas the post of Senior Medical Officer is lying vacant.
5.72 The Committee believes that in a country like India with acute shortage of medical
professionals, AYUSH through its traditional methods of treatment can go a long way in
providing basic health care facilities to the citizens. National AYUSH Mission was launched
in 2014 with the same vision, to provide cost effective and equitable AYUSH health care
2016-17 2017-till 25 November, 2018

80
throughout the country by improving access to AYUSH services. AIIMS Patna being the
foremost institute of medical care should understand the importance of medical pluralism
and make every effort to strengthen the AYUSH system. The Committee, therefore, strongly
recommends that other branches of AYUSH should be operationalized at AIIMS Patna without
delay and recruitment to the sanctioned posts must be carried out immediately.
Community Outreach Services
5.73 AIIMS Patna informed that it has been providing Community Outreach Services in the form
of Medical camps. It conducts around 15-20 camps in a year to provide medical consultation in
remote areas. AIIMS Patna also provides tele-consultation facility to patients in remote areas
through Mobile application developed by AIIMS Patna. Till September, 2018, 6128 consultations via
telemedicine have been provided.
5.74 The Committee appreciates AIIMS Patna in its initiative of providing medical consultation
through telecommunications and holding of medical camps in remote areas which has benefited
patients from far flung areas of the State. The Committee feels that AIIMS Patna has set
an example for other AIIMS like Institutions for their initiative with respect to community
outreach programs like telemedicine and accordingly recommends that other new AIIMS to
start similar programs and mobile applications for their respective regions.
Governing Body
5.75 AIIMS Patna in its written submission has insisted that the Governing Body of the Institute
should meet at least once in a year. The Committee has been informed that so far only two
meetings of the Governing Body have been conducted, the first meeting was held on 19th September,
2016 and the second meeting on 5th December, 2017. Governing Body consists of the honourable
members of Parliament and representatives from the Ministry of Health and Family Welfare and the
State. The Committee notes that the Chief Secretary of Bihar is the member of the Governing Body
of AIIMS Patna but he did not attend any of the past two meetings of the Governing body.
5.76 The Committee strongly recommends that the meetings of the Governing body, Academic
Committee and Standing Finance Committee should be held frequently to discharge its
entrusted responsibilities. The Committee also notes that the course of action decided for
some agenda in the first meeting of the Governing Body had not been implemented even
11104 12078

81
after one year and was again taken up in the second meeting of the Governing Body. The
Committee, therefore, feels that the Ministry must devise a follow up plan for the agenda
discussed in such meetings. The Ministry must ensure that the decisions of the Governing
body/Academic Committee/Standing Finance Committees are speedily implemented at the
ground level and undue delays are avoided. The Committee also urges the State Government
of Bihar to play a more active role in making AIIMS Patna a centre of excellence in
medical learning and healthcare treatment.
Monitoring Mechanism
5.77 The Committee is of the considered view that the success of any project lies in
effective implementation and evaluation. State Project Monitoring Committee (State PMC)
were entrusted with the responsibility to monitor the progress of new AIIMS in the State.
This Committee was required to meet at least once in a quarter to review the progress of
AIIMS. In the State of Bihar no such State project Monitoring Committee (State PMC) has
been constituted and in the absence of the required mechanism, no meetings could be
conducted. The Committee feels that the various issues plaguing AIIMS Patna could have
been easily solved had the concept of State PMC was implemented on ground.
5.78 AIIMS Patna, an Institute of National Importance today trails behind in many areas
that are fundamental for providing quality healthcare. The Committee expects AIIMS Patna
to make extra efforts to build itself into a world class institute as envisioned while establishing
the institute.

CHAPTER-VI
AIIMS, RAIPUR
6.1 AIIMS Raipur was started on 24th September, 2012 and is scheduled to be fully functional
with all super-specialties and Operation Theatres so as to because the centre of excellence for a
medical teaching and serve the tertiary care needs as a referral center for the people of Chhattisgarh
and nearby States.
6.2 The  Committee has been informed  that average OPD footfall is 1300 patients and total IPD
admissions in 2018 till 30th September, 2018 was 7528 with  600 bed strength. It was also
informed that the ratio of patient to bed is 1:2 and doctor-patient ratio is 1:7 in 2018.
BUDGETARY ALLOCATION

to AIIMS, Raipur since the beginning of the Institute:-

6.4 The Committee observes that optimum utilization of the funds to achieve the set objectives
has not been done in AIIMS, Raipur. The percentage of utilization was only 23.7% in 2012-13 that
increased to 36.66% in 2013-14 and 47.95% in 2014-15 but subsequently declined to 43.79% and
37.32% in 2015-16 and 2016-17 respectively. The percentage utilization of the available funds has
increased to 56.19% in 2017-18 and 48.65% till 30th September, 2018 for 2018-19.
6.5 The Committee was given to understand that there has not been any dearth of funds for
ongoing construction projects like Academic Building, Administrative Building, School of Public
Health (SPH) and Residential Phase-II for the year 2018-19 and 2019-20.  The Committee was
apprised about the following reasons for under utilisation of funds:-
82

83
z In the year 2015-16, as against budget allocation/sanction of `79.00 crore under GIA-
CCA, the fund released was `118.00 crore while utilization was `57.42 crore as the
project work had slowed down because of technical problems and non-availability of
Project Consultant.
z In the year 2016-17, as against budget allocation/sanction of `129.26 crore under GIA-
CCA, the fund released was `158.07 crore while utilization was `78.20 crore due to
delayed construction work.
z In the year 2017-18, as against budget allocation/sanction of `128.15 crore under GIA
Salaries, the `100.00 crore was released while utilisation was `61.77 crore. Expenditure
under the head Salaries could not be attained to the full extent as arrears to faculty
members could not be paid due to non-finalisation of anomalies in their salary structure
as per the 7th CPC recommendations (CCS RP Rules 2016).
z Previously, the financial power of Director was limited to `2 crore only, which resulted
in slowing down the expenditure. Now it has been enhanced to `5 crore which will
enhance the utilization of allocated fund. It was also mentioned that the financial power
of the Director, AIIMS Raipur could be further enhanced to `20 crore for capital
expenditure same in line with AIIMS New Delhi to achieve optimum utilization of funds.
6.6 The Committee observes with concern that the objectives of the PMSSY scheme are
not being realized in AIIMS, Raipur because of massive underutilization of funds in the
initial years. The utilization of the fund has constantly remained below 50 per cent in all
the years except for the year 2017-18 where it was 56.19 per cent. The Committee, further,
observes despite the fact that there was no dearth of funds in the initial years of construction
activities because of lack of absorption capacity of implementing agencies, technical problems
and non-availability of project consultants, the construction work got delayed. The released
funds under the head GIA (CCA) for two years i.e. 2015-16 and 2016-17 were more than
the funds sanctioned to the Institute. The main problem does not seem to be inadequate
budget outlay but poor utilization of funds by the Institute. The Committee observes that
the main reason for unspent funds was the slow progress of construction work. It is a
matter of great concern and, therefore, requires introspection to assess the reasons for
inability of the Institute in judicious utilization of funds.
6.7 The Committee believes that the under-utilisation of plan outlays by the Institute can
be attributed to the institutional and procedural bottlenecks in the process of implementation
of planned activities. The Committee also take into cognizance the observation of the CAG
Audit Report (2018) that pointed out the Ministry had not formulated any operational
guidelines for PMSSY, thereby, lacking in overall framework for scheme implementation.
Approval and adoption of ad-hoc approach in term of project planning and fund management,
delay in selection of consultant led to delay in establishment of the AIIMS. The institute
did not obtain clear title over the land provided by the State Government for construction
of residential complex and some Departments and centre of Institutes, that left the institute
open to further land disputes and encroachment. Needless to say, deficiencies in the planning
process and poor financial management in the Institute caused all these sorry state of
affairs. The Committee is of the view that effective financial management along with prudent

84
budgetary projection would have enabled optimum utilisation of funds. The Committee,
therefore, recommends the Institute to strengthen its financial management for optimum
utilization of financial resources at the Institute. The Committee, further, recommends to
the Institute to have effective monitoring and supervision over the implementing agencies
so as to avoid underutilization and idling of funds.
